Document Control
Volume 5 is the controlled AI architecture of The Eye. It governs every model, method, 
agent, prompt, context, retrieval, tool, evaluator, learned artifact, AI product, safety 
control, and AI operating process inside the frozen Strategic Intelligence Operating 
System.
CONTROL VALUE
Product The Eye
Tagline The Operating System for Strategic Intelligence
Controlled document Volume 5 — AI Architecture
Edition 1.0
Issue date 1 August 2026
Status Controlled AI Architecture Baseline
Binding sources Volume 0 — Product Constitution v1.0; Volume 3 — Technical Architecture v1.0; Volume 4 — 
Engineering Specification v1.0
Primary audience CTO, Chief AI Officer, principal architects and engineers, AI and data engineering, model risk, 
security, privacy, SRE, governance, domain leadership, product and programme leadership
Normative scope AI system planes; model and inference platform; context and grounding; multi-agent runtime; named 
agent architecture; safety; explainability; evaluation; learning; release; deployment; incidents
Downstream inheritance Volume 6 — Infrastructure Architecture; Volume 7 — Data Platform; model and agent specifications; 
evaluation plans; safety cases; implementation repositories and operating procedures
Normative language
SHALL, MUST, SHALL NOT, and MUST NOT are binding. MAY permits an implementation choice inside 
a frozen semantic and authority boundary. Requirements carry stable AI-CC-NNN identities. 
Conformance is scoped to the exact task, artifact, configuration, data, population, consequence class, 
environment, deployment profile, and period under review.
CONTROLLING RULE
Volume 0 controls constitutional meaning. Volume 3 controls product architecture. Volume 4 
controls engineering realization. Volume 5 controls AI architecture beneath those authorities. No 
model, agent, evaluator, or provider can create a parallel truth system or final decision authority.

AI Architecture Charter
The Eye uses artificial intelligence to extend human strategic judgment while keeping 
institutional authority, truth, memory, evidence, policy, and accountability outside the 
control of any model or agent.
AI COMMITMENT BINDING ARCHITECTURE CONSEQUENCE
Humans decide AI recommends, forecasts, simulates, reasons, explains, and prepares; named humans retain 
final authority for consequential decisions and irreversible effects.
Ground before inference Context resolves through governed evidence, Enterprise Memory, the Knowledge Graph, 
temporal state, and Digital Twins before decision-active products are admitted.
Explain by construction Every material result carries evidence, method, assumptions, uncertainty, alternatives, dissent, 
limitations, policy, and execution lineage.
Models are plural 
dependencies
Task and product contracts remain stable while approved providers, model families, rules, 
statistics, graph methods, optimizers, and simulators evolve.
Agents are bounded runtime 
packages
Identity, role, task, context, models, tools, authority, budgets, supervision, gates, failure, 
evidence, and lifecycle are explicit.
No silent learning Feedback and outcomes produce governed change proposals; production behavior changes 
only through evaluated, approved, reversible releases.
Trust is enforced outside AI Policy, capabilities, side-effect boundaries, human gates, audit, safety monitoring, and 
emergency stop do not depend on model compliance.
One product across 
deployments
SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise preserve the same task, product, authority, provenance, 
uncertainty, safety, replay, and lifecycle semantics.
Definition of AI architecture complete
• Every AI capability maps to one canonical layer, owning component, task contract, product schema, 
consequence class, accountable owner, and evidence profile.
• Every execution can reconstruct its instructions, context, retrieval, models, agents, tools, policies, 
evaluations, human actions, outputs, effects, and terminal state.
• Uncertainty, abstention, partial results, dissent, correction, degraded service, and revocation remain 
explicit and testable product states.
• Agent and model changes pass scope-specific evaluation, red teaming, safety, privacy, security, 
deployment-parity, release, monitoring, rollback, and retirement controls.
• Decision Replay preserves the original information and authority boundary while later outcomes 
and lessons remain distinct comparison state.

Executive AI Architecture Statement
Volume 5 defines the governed intelligence machinery that allows The Eye to observe, 
understand, predict, simulate, recommend, explain, and learn continuously while 
preserving human authority and institutional accountability.
The AI architecture is not a chatbot framework and it is not a collection of autonomous digital employees. 
It is a controlled computational system embedded in the ten canonical layers. Models, agents, graph 
methods, temporal methods, optimizers, simulators, retrieval systems, and rules operate as plural, 
replaceable dependencies behind stable strategic product contracts. The Knowledge Graph, Enterprise 
Memory, Digital Twins, evidence, policy, and temporal truth provide grounding; owning layers admit 
canonical state; named humans decide.
Figure 1. The governed AI operating loop. AI capabilities remain grounded in the Knowledge Graph and subordinate to human final 
authority.
AI PLANE ARCHITECTURE OBLIGATION
Model control plane Own task contracts, artifact identity, resolution, adapters, instructions, inference security, 
structured output, capacity, and economics.
Agent control plane Own agent packages, planning, supervision, durable workflow, messages, capabilities, tools, 
human gates, checkpoints, and replay.
Context control plane Assemble minimum authorized temporal context from retrieval, graph, memory, twins, evidence, 
and policy with a reproducible manifest.
AI product plane Produce typed evidence-bound assessments, forecasts, scenarios, simulations, warnings, 
explanations, and decision packages for owning-layer admission.
Trust and governance 
plane
Classify risk, enforce privacy and security, constrain effects, preserve audit, enable contest, and 
keep safety controls external to AI.
Evaluation and learning 
plane
Establish scope-specific fitness, monitor drift and outcomes, govern change, release immutable 
configurations, and retire or revoke safely.
AI ARCHITECTURE THESIS
The Eye gains strategic power by connecting methods, evidence, memory, graph, twins, futures, 
and human decisions into one controlled operating loop. It remains trustworthy only when 
uncertainty, disagreement, authority, effects, correction, and learning are explicit state—not implied 
behavior.

1  AI Architecture Authority and Scope
Volume 5 defines the complete artificial-intelligence architecture of The Eye within the 
frozen product constitution, ten-layer technical architecture, and controlled engineering 
baseline. It governs models, agents, context, tools, evaluation, safety, learning, and AI 
operations without creating an autonomous decision system or an alternate source of 
strategic truth.
Figure 2. AI Architecture Authority and Scope. The diagram organizes authority, applies to, product boundary, conformance result 
as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Authority Volume 0 controls constitutional meaning; Volume 3 controls product architecture; Volume 4 
controls engineering realization; Volume 5 controls AI architecture.
Applies to All model families, agent packages, prompts, instructions, retrieval, context assembly, tools, 
evaluators, learned artifacts, AI products, and AI operating controls.
Product boundary AI capabilities operate inside the ten canonical layers and shared control services; they are not an 
eleventh layer, a separate copilot product, or a decision authority.
Conformance result Conformant, conditionally conformant under a recorded exception, or non-conformant for an exact 
artifact, task, consequence class, customer domain, and deployment profile.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-01-001
Every AI capability SHALL identify its owning canonical layer, V3 
logical component, V4 deployable owner, task contract, 
consequence class, product schema, control profile, evaluation 
profile, and accountable human owner.
Security / privacy 
test
Signed adversarial result bound to 
task, artifacts, data class, provider, 
deployment, and policy.
AI-01-002
No model, agent, evaluator, retrieval service, or learned artifact 
SHALL establish independent strategic truth, final decision 
authority, policy authority, or canonical state ownership.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
AI-01-003
AI output SHALL enter shared product state only through the 
owning component's admission contract with explicit truth state, 
uncertainty, provenance, policy, quality, and lifecycle.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.
AI-01-004
An implementation SHALL NOT represent itself as The Eye when 
it bypasses human final authority, temporal truth, end-to-end 
provenance, tenant isolation, deployment semantic parity, or 
governed learning.
Security / privacy 
test
Signed adversarial result bound to 
task, artifacts, data class, provider, 
deployment, and policy.
AI-01-005
Production release SHALL be blocked when the applicable AI 
architecture, engineering, evaluation, safety, data, deployment, 
or operational evidence is absent, stale, or bound to another 
artifact version.
Evaluation / 
monitoring
Reproducible profile, evidence set, 
slice metrics, thresholds, uncertainty, 
reviewer, and fitness decision.

Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Unmapped AI capability Deny production registration until layer, component, task, owner, controls, and evidence are 
recorded.
Conflict with higher authority Stop promotion and resolve through constitutional or architecture change control; local AI 
governance cannot waive the conflict.
Undeclared AI behavior Disable or quarantine the behavior, identify affected products and decisions, and initiate 
conformance review.
Assurance evidence
• AI inventory resolves every production artifact to canonical layer, component, object, interface, 
owner, and release manifest.
• End-to-end fixtures prove model and agent outputs cannot bypass canonical admission or human 
authority.
• Architecture review finds no AI-owned parallel truth, policy, decision, or cross-deployment semantic 
fork.
• Production registry rejects unsigned, unmapped, unfit, expired, or revoked AI artifacts.
CHAPTER ACCEPTANCE AND INHERITANCE
AI Architecture Authority and Scope is conformant only when every requirement is implemented for 
the declared scope, failure states are observable and recoverable, and evidence resolves to the 
exact released configuration. Primary Volume 4 engineering anchors: Ch. 1, Ch. 2, Ch. 4, Ch. 6, 
Ch. 14, Ch. 43, Ch. 45, Ch. 71, Ch. 72.

2  AI Specification Hierarchy and Conformance
AI behavior is controlled through a versioned authority chain. Task specifications, 
evaluation profiles, prompts, model policies, agent manifests, and learned artifacts add 
precision beneath Volume 5 but may not silently weaken its semantics or the authorities 
above it.
Figure 3. AI Specification Hierarchy and Conformance. The diagram organizes control order, conformance unit, compatibility, 
exception as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Control order Constitution -> Technical Architecture -> Engineering Specification -> AI Architecture -> AI domain 
and task specifications -> artifacts -> evidence.
Conformance unit One exact combination of artifact, task, data class, consequence, policy, customer domain, 
environment, and deployment profile.
Compatibility Wire compatibility is insufficient; semantic, policy, temporal, uncertainty, explanation, and side-effect 
compatibility are required.
Exception Narrow, owned, risk-accepted, time-bounded, monitored, reversible, and prohibited from changing 
human or constitutional authority.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-02-001
Every AI artifact SHALL declare controlling specifications, owner, 
status, version, effective time, task scope, consequence scope, 
evaluation expiry, dependencies, and supersession.
Evaluation / 
monitoring
Reproducible profile, evidence set, 
slice metrics, thresholds, uncertainty, 
reviewer, and fitness decision.
AI-02-002
Conformance SHALL be re-evaluated when model, agent, 
prompt, context source, retrieval method, tool, policy, schema, 
evaluation population, deployment, or external dependency 
changes materially.
Evaluation / 
monitoring
Reproducible profile, evidence set, 
slice metrics, thresholds, uncertainty, 
reviewer, and fitness decision.
AI-02-003
Derived AI specifications SHALL reference parent requirements 
and SHALL NOT replace explicit uncertainty, provenance, 
abstention, human gate, or deployment-parity obligations with 
aggregate quality claims.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
AI-02-004
Conditional conformance SHALL identify the exact affected 
tenants, tasks, products, decisions, controls, monitoring, expiry, 
and closure criteria.
Security / privacy 
test
Signed adversarial result bound to 
task, artifacts, data class, provider, 
deployment, and policy.
AI-02-005
Expired, revoked, or indeterminate conformance SHALL stop 
new resolution of the affected artifact and initiate impact analysis 
for active runs and decision-active products.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.

Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Conflicting AI artifacts The higher controlling version prevails; quarantine the lower artifact and its dependent release.
Evidence cannot be reproduced Return indeterminate conformance and prohibit promotion or scope expansion.
Exception scope expands Mark non-conformant, stop affected use, and require explicit amendment or closure.
Assurance evidence
• Machine-readable manifests prove closed references across specifications, artifacts, tasks, 
policies, evaluations, and releases.
• Compatibility suites cover meaning, uncertainty, policy, provenance, side effects, human gates, and 
degraded behavior.
• Exception registry proves independent approval, compensating controls, telemetry, expiry, and non-
expansion.
• Historical resolution reconstructs the exact conformance state effective for any AI product or 
decision replay.

3  Human Decision Sovereignty
AI may recommend, forecast, simulate, reason, explain, prioritize, draft, and prepare. A 
named human retains final authority for consequential institutional decisions, material 
commitments, irreversible effects, and changes to the authority system itself.
Figure 4. Human Decision Sovereignty. The diagram organizes ai authority, human authority, decision boundary, non-delegable acts 
as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
AI authority Bounded analytical and operational preparation under purpose, task, data, tool, model, budget, 
policy, supervision, and stop conditions.
Human authority Authenticated role with current mandate to decide, approve, reject, abstain, delegate, challenge, 
reopen, or withdraw.
Decision boundary The exact point at which a recommendation becomes an institutional decision or an external side 
effect becomes authorized.
Non-delegable acts Final high-impact decision, approval of irreversible effect, change to policy authority, and 
acceptance of unresolved material risk where policy requires a human.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-03-001
Every consequential AI-assisted decision SHALL terminate at a 
durable human gate that presents the exact proposed act, 
alternatives, evidence, uncertainty, dissent, policy, expected 
effects, reversibility, and missing information.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
AI-03-002
Model confidence, agent consensus, elapsed time, workflow 
timeout, system administration, service identity, or prior approval 
SHALL NOT be interpreted as current human consent.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
AI-03-003
AI MAY propose an action or decision but SHALL NOT broaden 
its scope, alter the approved effect, select the approving human, 
or execute an approval-required effect after the gate context 
changes.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
AI-03-004
The human decision record SHALL bind the approver, authority, 
information boundary, exact choice, rationale, reservations, 
overrides, obligations, time, and downstream commitment state.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
AI-03-005
Emergency operation SHALL preserve named human command, 
bounded authority, independent audit, later reconciliation, and 
explicit prohibition against silent conversion into normal 
autonomous behavior.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.

Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Authorized human unavailable Escalate or reassign under policy; elapsed time produces expiry or suspension, never approval.
Decision package materially changes Invalidate the pending act and require refreshed human review of the changed boundary.
AI attempts prohibited effect Deny at the tool and workflow enforcement points, terminate or suspend the execution, and open 
safety review.
Assurance evidence
• Human-gate suites cover role, delegation, quorum, separation, stale context, revocation, expiry, 
emergency, and replay.
• Adversarial tests prove models, agents, administrators, and service accounts cannot synthesize or 
reuse human approval.
• Decision Replay reconstructs exactly what the human saw, what AI produced, and which authority 
applied.
• Side-effect receipts prove the dispatched action matches the approved request digest and 
permitted execution window.
CHAPTER ACCEPTANCE AND INHERITANCE

44, Ch. 48, Ch. 55.

4  Explainability by Construction
Explanation is produced from the same evidence, models, assumptions, tools, policies, 
and transformations that produced the recommendation. It is not a persuasive narrative 
generated after the decision boundary and it never conceals uncertainty or 
disagreement.
Figure 5. Explainability by Construction. The diagram organizes explanation product, mechanistic evidence, epistemic explanation, 
audience adaptation as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Explanation product Versioned, policy-filtered object linking conclusion, evidence, method, assumptions, alternatives, 
uncertainty, limitations, dissent, and responsible owners.
Mechanistic evidence Execution trace, retrieval manifest, model and agent versions, tool results, transformation lineage, 
and governing policy decisions.
Epistemic explanation What is known, claimed, inferred, assessed, simulated, uncertain, contradicted, missing, or out of 
distribution.
Audience adaptation Role-appropriate language and detail that preserves the same meaning, caveats, evidence, and 
decision-use constraints.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-04-001
Every recommendation, forecast, scenario, simulation 
interpretation, warning, score, and decision package SHALL 
carry an explanation product derived from preserved execution 
and provenance state.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
AI-04-002
Explanations SHALL distinguish source evidence, deterministic 
transformation, model inference, agent judgment, human 
assessment, assumptions, synthetic state, and later correction.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
AI-04-003
An explanation SHALL expose material counter-evidence, 
competing hypotheses, model or agent disagreement, sensitivity, 
failure conditions, abstention, and unknowns.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, checkpoint, product 
state, effect reconciliation, recovery, 
and accountable closure.
AI-04-004
Natural-language rendering MAY summarize an explanation but 
SHALL NOT introduce unsupported reasons, remove material 
limitations, or imply access to hidden model cognition.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied, 
observable, and audited.
AI-04-005
Explanation completeness and faithfulness SHALL be evaluated 
for each task and consequence class and SHALL constrain 
decision-active use when below threshold.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.

Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Required lineage missing Return explanation incomplete, mark the product unfit for consequential use, and repair or 
withdraw it.
Explanation conflicts with trace Treat as a faithfulness failure, preserve both artifacts, and initiate product impact review.
Audience lacks access to evidence Provide a policy-safe explanation with explicit withheld scope; do not invent substitute 
justification.
Assurance evidence
• Golden traces compare explanation claims with exact inputs, retrieval, tools, models, policies, and 
output transformations.
• Counterfactual tests change material evidence and verify the explanation and recommendation 
respond coherently.
• Human review measures comprehensibility, contestability, uncertainty communication, and decision 
usefulness by role.
• Security tests prove explanations do not disclose unauthorized content, hidden prompts, secrets, or 
cross-domain information.
CHAPTER ACCEPTANCE AND INHERITANCE
Explainability by Construction is conformant only when every requirement is implemented for the 
declared scope, failure states are observable and recoverable, and evidence resolves to the exact 
released configuration. Primary Volume 4 engineering anchors: Ch. 20, Ch. 21, Ch. 24, Ch. 27, Ch. 
28, Ch. 39, Ch. 46, Ch. 55, Ch. 57.

5  Model, Method, and Agent Pluralism
The Eye is model- and vendor-plural while preserving one strategic product contract. 
Models, rules, statistical methods, graph algorithms, optimizers, simulators, retrieval 
systems, and agents are replaceable governed dependencies selected by task fitness 
and deployment policy.
Figure 6. Model, Method, and Agent Pluralism. The diagram organizes pluralism, resolution, composition, portability as one 
governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Pluralism More than one approved computational method may satisfy a task contract without changing 
canonical product meaning.
Resolution Policy-bound selection using task, consequence, data, deployment, sovereignty, quality, latency, 
cost, capacity, and availability constraints.
Composition Declared ensemble, cascade, debate, retrieval, tool, or human-review pattern with preserved 
component results and aggregation method.
Portability Canonical contracts remain independent of provider-specific prompts, tokens, embeddings, tool 
formats, safety labels, and operational APIs.
Normative requirements

AI architecture SHALL separate task contracts and canonical 
products from provider-specific model, agent, retrieval, 
embedding, and tool interfaces.

for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.

fitness, data rights, sovereignty, safety, compatibility, and 
operational approval for the requested scope.

independent authority, scope,

for exact task, artifacts, context,

6  AI Traceability and Documentation Inheritance
Every AI product must be traversable from constitutional authority to runtime execution 
and back. Traceability binds requirements, canonical layers, components, tasks, 
schemas, models, agents, prompts, context, tools, policies, evaluations, releases, 
decisions, outcomes, and later learning.
Figure 7. AI Traceability and Documentation Inheritance. The diagram organizes upward trace, downward trace, impact trace, 
historical trace as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Upward trace AI artifact and behavior -> V5 requirement -> V4 engineering contract -> V3 architecture -> V0 
invariant.
Downward trace Authority -> task and product contract -> artifacts and configuration -> tests and release -> runtime 
evidence and outcomes.
Impact trace Changed source, model, agent, prompt, policy, tool, context, schema, evaluation, or package -> 
affected products, decisions, and deployments.
Historical trace Exact versions and effective authority resolved for any execution, product, human decision, incident, 
correction, or replay time.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-06-001
Every normative AI requirement SHALL have a stable identity, 
owning authority, verification method, required evidence, 
implementation mapping, and lifecycle status.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
AI-06-002
Every production AI execution SHALL preserve references to 
task, product schema, model, agent, prompt, context, retrieval, 
tool, policy, evaluation, configuration, release, and deployment 
versions.
Evaluation / 
monitoring
Reproducible profile, evidence set, 
slice metrics, thresholds, uncertainty, 
reviewer, and fitness decision.
AI-06-003
Traceability SHALL support backward explanation and forward 
impact analysis across synchronous, event, workflow, agent, 
model, tool, and human boundaries.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
AI-06-004
A change to any decision-relevant AI dependency SHALL identify 
and classify affected products as current, stale, challenged, 
withdrawn, recomputation-required, or human-review-required.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
AI-06-005
Documentation, manifests, registries, schemas, and test 
evidence SHALL be released as controlled artifacts and SHALL 
remain historically resolvable after supersession.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.

7  AI System Context
The AI subsystem is an embedded reasoning and execution fabric serving the ten 
canonical layers. It receives governed tasks and context, invokes approved models and 
agents, returns typed intelligence products, and remains bounded by canonical state 
owners, human authority, and cross-cutting controls.
Figure 8. AI System Context. The diagram organizes callers, ai boundary, state boundary, outputs as one governed AI architecture 
contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Callers Canonical layer components, durable workflows, governed human workspaces, evaluation systems, 
and approved external integrations.
AI boundary Model gateway, context services, agent runtime, tool broker, evaluation plane, safety controls, and 
artifact registries.
State boundary AI registries and execution evidence are authoritative for AI operation; strategic truth remains with 
canonical layer owners.
Outputs Typed proposals, transformations, assessments, forecasts, scenarios, simulations, 
recommendations, explanations, warnings, and evaluation evidence.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-07-001
AI services SHALL accept work only through authenticated, 
authorized, versioned task contracts carrying purpose, tenant, 
domain, consequence, deadline, data, side-effect, and human-
gate context.
Security / privacy 
test
Signed adversarial result bound to 
task, artifacts, data class, provider, 
deployment, and policy.
AI-07-002
AI services SHALL consume canonical objects by immutable 
reference or policy-filtered projection and SHALL NOT write 
directly into another component's authoritative store.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied, 
observable, and audited.
AI-07-003
AI results SHALL be proposals or typed products whose owning 
canonical component validates and commits decision-active 
state.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
AI-07-004
External model, tool, search, and data providers SHALL remain 
outside the trusted customer domain until gateway, policy, 
provenance, minimization, integrity, and sovereignty controls 
complete.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.

AI system health SHALL distinguish model, agent, context, tool, 
evaluation, control, product quality, and decision-use state rather 
than exposing a single availability indicator.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.

8  AI Plane Decomposition
AI architecture is decomposed into interoperating operational planes beneath the 
canonical product layers. The decomposition creates clear ownership and control 
without introducing alternate product layers or shared mutable authority.
Figure 9. AI Plane Decomposition. The diagram organizes model plane, context plane, agent plane, assurance planes as one 
governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Model plane Model registry, adapters, routing, inference, embeddings, optimization, and model lifecycle.
Context plane Retrieval, memory access, graph and temporal grounding, twin context, policy filtering, and citation 
binding.
Agent plane Identity, planning, supervision, workflow, communication, tools, sandboxes, checkpoints, and human 
gates.
Assurance planes Evaluation, safety, policy, provenance, audit, observability, learning, release, deployment, and 
sovereignty.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-08-001
Each AI plane SHALL have one accountable platform owner, 
explicit state classes, versioned interfaces, deployment 
boundary, SLOs, failure domains, and conformance suites.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, checkpoint, product 
state, effect reconciliation, recovery, 
and accountable closure.
AI-08-002
Plane boundaries SHALL use canonical envelopes and SHALL 
prohibit shared-write databases, ambient context, implicit tenant 
state, and provider-specific semantics outside adapters.
Security / privacy 
test
Signed adversarial result bound to 
task, artifacts, data class, provider, 
deployment, and policy.
AI-08-003
Task execution SHALL compose planes through a durable 
execution manifest that identifies every resolved dependency and 
control decision.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
AI-08-004
Control and assurance planes SHALL remain independently 
enforceable and observable from model, agent, context, and tool 
implementations.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.
AI-08-005
Plane consolidation into a deployable unit MAY occur only when 
state authority, telemetry, policy, resource, failure, and audit 
boundaries remain independently testable.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, checkpoint, product 
state, effect reconciliation, recovery, 
and accountable closure.

FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Ownership overlap Assign a single state owner and replace shared writes with declared contracts or rebuildable 
projections.

9  AI Product and Value Flow
AI work begins with a governed task and ends with a canonical product decision, not 
with raw model output. The flow preserves intent, evidence, context, methods, 
uncertainty, explanation, review, admission, use, correction, and outcome through one 
causally connected execution record.
Figure 10. AI Product and Value Flow. The diagram organizes task, execution, candidate product, admitted product as one 
governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Task Declared analytical objective, caller, purpose, consequence, inputs, context policy, output schema, 
quality, deadline, and side-effect class.
Execution Resolved plan, artifacts, context, tools, controls, intermediate products, evaluations, challenges, and 
terminal state.
Candidate product Schema-valid proposed intelligence object with truth state, uncertainty, provenance, policy, quality, 
and explanation.
Admitted product Owning component's committed version with lifecycle, consumer constraints, monitoring, correction, 
and replay references.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-09-001
AI task admission SHALL bind the exact requested product 
schema, consequence class, quality threshold, explanation 
profile, human review, and permitted side effects before 
execution.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
AI-09-002
Intermediate model and agent outputs SHALL remain execution 
evidence and SHALL NOT become shared memory or graph 
truth without owning-component admission.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied, 
observable, and audited.
AI-09-003
Candidate products SHALL distinguish observed, claimed, 
inferred, assessed, synthetic, simulated, recommended, decided, 
withdrawn, and superseded state as applicable.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.
AI-09-004
Product admission SHALL evaluate schema, evidence, temporal 
scope, provenance, uncertainty, policy, quality, duplication, 
contradiction, correction, and accountable ownership.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.
AI-09-005
Use, challenge, override, correction, decision, commitment, and 
outcome events SHALL link back to the admitted product and 
initiate evaluation or governed learning where applicable.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.

Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Task cannot satisfy output contract Return abstained, partial, or failed state with unsatisfied conditions; do not fabricate a complete 
product.
Candidate validation fails Quarantine execution output and preserve raw evidence for correction or investigation.
Admitted product later invalidated Propagate stale, challenged, withdrawn, or recomputation-required state to dependent 
consumers and decisions.
Assurance evidence
• End-to-end golden cases trace tasks through execution, candidate validation, admission, use, 
correction, and outcome.
• Negative tests prove raw model and agent output cannot enter canonical stores through caches, 
logs, vectors, or user surfaces.
• Correction fixtures identify every dependent context, product, warning, decision, briefing, and 
learning proposal.
• Replay reconstructs the exact product versions, information boundary, AI execution, and human 
review effective at use time.

10  Model Control Plane
The model control plane governs model identity, provenance, task fitness, policy, 
compatibility, resolution, release, monitoring, withdrawal, and historical use across first-
party, customer-provided, open, embedded, and external models.
Figure 11. Model Control Plane. The diagram organizes registry, policy, resolution, lifecycle as one governed AI architecture 
contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Registry Authoritative model profiles, versions, composition, provenance, rights, evaluations, task approvals, 
constraints, and lifecycle.
Policy Permitted tasks, data classes, domains, deployments, regions, providers, context, retention, logging, 
fallback, and consequence.
Resolution Deterministic selection of an approved model or ensemble for an exact invocation contract.
Lifecycle Proposed, quarantined, evaluated, approved, shadow, constrained, active, suspended, withdrawn, 
deprecated, or retired.

11  Agent Control Plane
The agent control plane governs agent identity, package provenance, role, task scope, 
capability grants, supervision, evaluation, release, monitoring, suspension, and 
revocation. It never grants an agent authority to redefine its own purpose or controls.
Figure 12. Agent Control Plane. The diagram organizes agent registry, capability policy, supervision policy, lifecycle as one 
governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Agent registry Signed package, role, owner, version, instructions, models, tools, data scopes, budgets, 
evaluations, deployment support, and lifecycle.
Capability policy Task-specific permitted data, tools, models, network, compute, storage, side effects, duration, and 
human gates.
Supervision policy Planner, supervisor, reviewer, escalation, challenge, checkpoint, stop, and terminal-state 
obligations.
Lifecycle Draft, quarantined, evaluated, approved, shadow, restricted, active, suspended, revoked, 
deprecated, or retired.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-11-001
Every agent SHALL execute under a signed registered package 
and a run-specific capability grant that is narrower than or equal 
to its approved role profile.
Security / privacy 
test
Signed adversarial result bound to 
task, artifacts, data class, provider, 
deployment, and policy.
AI-11-002
Agent packages SHALL declare instructions, models, tools, 
context sources, data classes, network destinations, side-effect 
classes, budgets, dependencies, evaluations, owners, and failure 
behavior.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, checkpoint, product 
state, effect reconciliation, recovery, 
and accountable closure.
AI-11-003
No agent SHALL create, approve, extend, or conceal its own 
capability grant, task purpose, human authority, evaluation 
status, or lifecycle state.
Security / privacy 
test
Signed adversarial result bound to 
task, artifacts, data class, provider, 
deployment, and policy.
AI-11-004
The control plane SHALL support immediate suspension and 
revocation and SHALL define treatment of active executions, 
credentials, checkpoints, products, and derived decisions.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, checkpoint, product 
state, effect reconciliation, recovery, 
and accountable closure.
AI-11-005

audit, and Decision Replay.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, checkpoint, product 
state, effect reconciliation, recovery, 
and accountable closure.

FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR

12  Context Control Plane
The context control plane assembles the exact policy-filtered information boundary 
presented to a model or agent. It controls source authority, temporal scope, truth state, 
retrieval method, ranking, coverage, token or feature budgets, citations, and context 
retention.
Figure 13. Context Control Plane. The diagram organizes context policy, context manifest, assembly, boundary result as one 
governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Context policy Allowed sources, object types, truth states, time, graph scope, twin state, data class, purpose, 
model, agent, retention, and export.
Context manifest Immutable ordered inventory of every object, excerpt, graph path, feature, tool result, 
transformation, omission, and version supplied.
Assembly Policy filter, retrieval, ranking, diversification, contradiction preservation, temporal resolution, 
minimization, formatting, and budget allocation.
Boundary result Complete, partial, stale, coverage-limited, policy-limited, unavailable, or challenged with explicit 
quality and use constraints.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-12-001
Every model and agent invocation SHALL bind an immutable 
context manifest sufficient to reconstruct the exact information 
boundary.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.
AI-12-002
Context assembly SHALL enforce tenant, purpose, classification, 
rights, residency, retention, truth state, temporal, and model or 
agent use policy before content enters the invocation.
Security / privacy 
test
Signed adversarial result bound to

connected state while preserving the distinct authority, truth, and lifecycle of each 
substrate.

strategy connections.
Twin grounding Observed, estimated, forecast, scenario, and simulated states with explicit branch, model, evidence, 
validity, and uncertainty.
Grounding manifest Exact revisions, snapshots, query semantics, projections, policies, watermarks, and quality supplied 
to an execution.

14  Evaluation, Governance, and Learning Planes
Evaluation, governance, and learning are independent control planes around AI 
production. Evaluation determines fitness, governance authorizes use and change, and 
learning proposes evidence-backed improvement; none of them silently modifies 
production behavior.
Figure 15. Evaluation, Governance, and Learning Planes. The diagram organizes evaluation plane, governance plane, learning 
plane, separation as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Evaluation plane Task profiles, evidence sets, metrics, challenge methods, runners, judges, fitness decisions, runtime 
monitors, and expiry.
Governance plane Authorities, approvals, exceptions, risk acceptance, policy, accountability, audit, marketplace, 
deprecation, and change control.
Learning plane Outcomes, corrections, feedback, overrides, incidents, lesson proposals, curation, training, 
evaluation, release, and rollback.
Separation Artifact creators, evaluators, risk acceptors, release approvers, and production operators remain 
independently attributable where consequence requires.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

and fitness decision for the exact artifact composition, population, 
consequence, and deployment scope.

independent authority, scope,

15  Model Identity, Registry, and Lifecycle
A model is a governed architecture artifact with stable identity and a task-specific 
lifecycle. Registry state connects model bytes or external versions to provenance, 
architecture, data terms, evaluations, policies, deployments, owners, incidents, derived 
products, and retirement obligations.
Figure 16. Model Identity, Registry, and Lifecycle. The diagram organizes identity, provenance, task profile, lifecycle as one 
governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Identity Stable model ID plus immutable version digest, provider identifier, architecture class, tokenizer or 
feature contract, and packaging format.
Provenance Publisher, source, training and adaptation lineage, build environment, composition, dependencies, 
licenses, attestations, and custody.
Task profile Approved tasks, populations, consequence classes, inputs, outputs, limitations, evaluation, 
explanation, and human review.
Lifecycle Submitted -> quarantined -> evaluated -> approved/shadow/constrained -> active -> 
suspended/withdrawn -> deprecated -> retired.

16  Model Gateway, Routing, and Resolution
The Model Gateway is the exclusive inference boundary. It normalizes task intent, 
evaluates policy and fitness, resolves an approved execution path, enforces budgets 
and data controls, records complete invocation lineage, and returns typed status without 
exposing provider semantics to callers.

abstention rules.
Execution path Selected model or composition, adapter, endpoint, region, parameters, safety profile, credentials, 
and resource reservation.
Result Complete, partial, abstained, refused, filtered, timed out, unavailable, failed, or challenged with 
normalized usage and quality evidence.

19  Context Assembly and Budgeting
Context assembly converts governed information into a bounded model- or agent-ready 
representation. It maximizes decision value while preserving policy, evidence diversity, 
contradiction, temporal validity, provenance, uncertainty, and known omissions.
Figure 20. Context Assembly and Budgeting. The diagram organizes assembly plan, budget, context segment, coverage report as 
one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Assembly plan Task-derived source families, retrieval methods, filters, ranking, diversification, compression, 
ordering, and token or feature allocation.
Budget Maximum content, history, tools, images, features, latency, cost, and protected-data exposure by 
context family.
Context segment Typed content plus source object, version, temporal scope, truth state, classification, quality, rank 
reason, and citation key.
Coverage report Requested and retrieved scope, omissions, truncation, contradictions, diversity, freshness, and 
confidence in context sufficiency.
Normative requirements

independent authority, scope,

independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.

for exact task, artifacts, context,

20  Structured Output and Product Admission
AI outputs are untrusted until transformed into a canonical candidate and admitted by 
the owning component. Structured output separates model generation from schema 
validity, semantic validity, evidence sufficiency, policy compliance, quality, and product 
authority.
Figure 21. Structured Output and Product Admission. The diagram organizes output schema, validation chain, repair, admission 
decision as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Output schema Versioned fields, types, enums, units, cardinality, temporal rules, truth state, uncertainty, citations, 
and prohibited combinations.
Validation chain Parse, schema, semantic, policy, evidence, temporal, ontology, quality, duplication, contradiction, 
and ownership checks.
Repair Bounded deterministic or model-assisted correction that preserves original output, repair instruction, 
changes, and evaluation.
Admission decision Admit, admit-with-constraint, return for review, quarantine, abstain, reject, or withdraw for an exact 
candidate version.
Normative requirements

authoritative state.

21  Inference Security and Privacy Boundary
Inference is a high-value trust boundary connecting protected context, executable 
instructions, model artifacts, external providers, tool schemas, output, telemetry, and 
billing. Security and privacy controls constrain data before invocation and treat all 
returned content as untrusted.
Figure 22. Inference Security and Privacy Boundary. The diagram organizes ingress controls, execution controls, egress controls, 
evidence as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Ingress controls Identity, purpose, authorization, classification, rights, minimization, residency, instruction integrity, 
schema, and malware/content checks.
Execution controls Workload identity, isolated endpoint, encrypted channel, key custody, provider policy, context 
retention, logging, and resource limits.
Egress controls Schema, policy, content safety, provenance, secrets, data loss, citation, classification, and 
destination checks.
Evidence Invocation digest, policy decisions, data manifest, endpoint identity, key references, provider receipt, 
output digest, and audit state.
Normative requirements

22  Inference Capacity, Latency, and Economics
Inference resources are allocated by strategic consequence, deadline, quality, and 
customer policy. Capacity and economics are observable constraints inside the task 
contract; cost or congestion may cause explicit degradation or abstention but never 
hidden quality loss or control bypass.
Figure 23. Inference Capacity, Latency, and Economics. The diagram organizes workload class, resource budget, priority, 
admission as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Workload class Interactive, warning, decision, workflow, agent, batch, embedding, evaluation, simulation, replay, or 
recovery inference.
Resource budget Tokens or features, calls, concurrency, accelerator, memory, storage, network, latency, cost, 
energy, and provider quota.
Priority Consequence, urgency, response window, customer policy, fairness, deadline, and recovery 
reserve.
Admission Accept, reserve, queue, batch, reduce optional work, choose conforming alternate, defer, or reject 
with visible reason.

Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Capacity exhausted Queue, defer, or return constrained/unavailable under declared priority; do not accept work

and after efficiency changes.

and recovery reserve.

23  Hybrid Retrieval Architecture
Hybrid retrieval combines lexical, semantic, graph, temporal, geospatial, structured, and 
source-native methods under one task and policy contract. It preserves method-specific 
evidence and uses fusion to improve coverage without erasing contradiction or retrieval 
uncertainty.
Figure 24. Hybrid Retrieval Architecture. The diagram organizes retrieval plan, candidate, fusion, result as one governed AI 
architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Retrieval plan Question decomposition, source classes, methods, filters, time, graph scope, geography, 
languages, depth, budgets, and stopping conditions.
Candidate Object or segment reference with method, rank, score, reason, source authority, version, truth state, 
time, quality, and policy.
Fusion Versioned normalization, deduplication, diversification, contradiction preservation, reranking, 
coverage assessment, and cutoff.
Result Ordered context candidates plus method contributions, coverage, omissions, freshness, projection 
watermarks, and retrieval uncertainty.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-23-001
Retrieval SHALL operate over governed object and projection 
references and SHALL NOT treat embeddings, indexes, cached 
snippets, or provider results as independent canonical truth.

conformance test

prohibited path is denied, 
observable, and audited.

type, language, temporal need, graph structure, source authority, 
consequence, and known failure modes.

exercise

state, effect reconciliation, recovery, 
and accountable closure.

contradiction, minority evidence, temporal scope, and policy

for exact task, artifacts, context,

24  Graph Retrieval and Graph Reasoning
Graph retrieval grounds AI in canonical identity, typed temporal relationships, 
provenance, ontology, and strategy context. Graph reasoning produces explicit paths, 
subgraphs, rules, hypotheses, and uncertainty that can be inspected and challenged.

admission as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract

provenance, and result limits.

algorithm with version.
Reasoning product Subgraph, paths, inferred relations, supporting and contradicting evidence, confidence, 
assumptions, limits, and revision watermark.
Admission Proposed inference remains distinct from observed or stewarded graph state until graph-owner 
validation and commit.

25  Enterprise, Strategic, and Agent Memory 
Boundaries
Enterprise Memory preserves governed institutional records; Strategic Memory 
connects evidence, assumptions, decisions, commitments, outcomes, and lessons; 
agent execution memory supports bounded work. These memory classes remain 
separate in authority, retention, reuse, and correction.
Figure 26. Enterprise, Strategic, and Agent Memory Boundaries. The diagram organizes enterprise memory, strategic memory, 
execution memory, experience memory as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Enterprise Memory Canonical governed organizational records owned by the Enterprise Memory layer.
Strategic Memory Policy-governed connected view of strategic evidence, assessments, objectives, decisions, 
commitments, outcomes, and lessons.
Execution memory Run-scoped plans, observations, tool results, checkpoints, intermediate products, reviews, and 
terminal evidence.
Experience memory Approved reusable task patterns, examples, procedures, and lessons released through governance; 
never silent episodic self-modification.

26  Temporal and Version-Aware Context
AI context preserves when events occurred, when sources were observed, when 
assertions applied, and when The Eye recorded them. Version-aware retrieval prevents 
current knowledge, corrected data, or later outcomes from contaminating historical 
forecasts, simulations, decisions, and replays.
Figure 27. Temporal and Version-Aware Context. The diagram organizes time dimensions, as-of boundary, version set, temporal 
product as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Time dimensions Event, observation, valid, system, issue, deadline, expiry, model training cutoff, and context 
assembly time.
As-of boundary Exact system-time view plus valid-time scope, policy version, projection watermark, and known 
clock uncertainty.
Version set Object, graph, twin, model, agent, prompt, retrieval, tool, policy, evaluation, and configuration 
versions supplied.
Temporal product Result whose inputs, applicability, horizon, freshness, expiry, and later correction state are explicit.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-26-001
Every AI task SHALL declare the temporal perspective required 
for inputs and outputs and SHALL reject ambiguous default-to-
current behavior for consequential work.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.
AI-26-002
Historical analysis SHALL resolve information by system time 
and SHALL keep later corrections, outcomes, and policy changes 
in a separate comparison boundary.
Evaluation / 
monitoring
Reproducible profile, evidence set, 
slice metrics, thresholds, uncertainty, 
reviewer, and fitness decision.
AI-26-003
Context assembly SHALL filter and label objects by event, 
observation, valid, and system time and SHALL expose clock 
quality, projection lag, and stale state.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.
AI-26-004
Model training cutoff and accessible knowledge horizon SHALL 
be declared where they materially affect novelty, leakage, or 
historical evaluation.
Evaluation / 
monitoring
Reproducible profile, evidence set, 
slice metrics, thresholds, uncertainty, 
reviewer, and fitness decision.
AI-26-005
Forecast, scenario, simulation, warning, and decision products 
SHALL carry creation time, target or valid horizon, expiry, refresh 
conditions, and dependency invalidation state.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.

FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Time boundary missing Return invalid temporal scope and require explicit time semantics.

• Decision Replay compares original and later knowledge without modifying the original information 
boundary.

stewardship state.

version.

human-stewardship required.

freshness, validity, and quality.
AI interaction Retrieve, reconcile proposal, estimate, validate, branch, forecast, simulate, challenge, explain, or 
monitor under declared authority.

Failure semantics

structural change.

29  Provenance, Citations, and Evidence Binding
Every material AI claim is bound to evidence and production lineage. Citations are 
resolvable pointers into governed source and object state, not decorative links or model-
generated references, and provenance supports both explanation and forward 
correction impact.

impact edge as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION

time, truth state, and policy.

transform, and admission.
Citation Audience-authorized stable reference with display label, source authority, temporal scope, access 
behavior, and integrity proof.
Impact edge Dependency from evidence or artifact to AI product, decision, commitment, export, or learned 
artifact.

30  Agent Identity, Role, and Package
An agent is a signed, versioned package that performs a bounded institutional role 
under an accountable owner. Agent identity binds instructions, models, tools, context 
sources, policies, evaluations, budgets, deployment support, and lifecycle so that 
behavior remains attributable and revocable.
Figure 31. Agent Identity, Role, and Package. The diagram organizes agent identity, role contract, package, lifecycle as one 
governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract

and compatibility.
Role contract Purpose, permitted tasks, product schemas, consequence classes, data, tools, models, side effects, 
supervision, and human gates.
Package Instructions, planners, policies, tool schemas, context profiles, model resolution, evaluators, 
configuration, tests, and documentation.
Lifecycle Draft, quarantined, evaluated, approved, shadow, constrained, active, suspended, revoked, 
deprecated, or retired.

31  Agent Execution Lifecycle
An agent execution is a durable, bounded, and auditable state machine. It begins with 
an admitted task and ends with an explicit product, abstention, failure, cancellation, 
compensation, or escalation state; an agent process ending is not institutional 
completion.
Figure 32. Agent Execution Lifecycle. The diagram organizes admission, execution graph, execution evidence, terminal states as 
one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Admission Task, actor, purpose, consequence, package, model policy, context policy, tools, budgets, gates, 
deadline, and stop conditions.
Execution graph Plans, tasks, dependencies, messages, tool calls, checkpoints, reviews, products, retries, 
compensation, and terminal state.
Execution evidence Immutable sequence of observations, decisions, versions, control results, usage, uncertainty, 
dissent, and side-effect receipts.
Terminal states Completed, partial, abstained, rejected, cancelled, expired, compensated, failed, or escalated-with-
residual-state.
Normative requirements

downstream agent work begins.

for exact task, artifacts, context,

Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Worker or process lost Resume from the last durable checkpoint without repeating acknowledged effects.
External effect uncertain Enter reconciliation-required state and prohibit retry until receipt or compensation resolves the 
boundary.
Terminal evidence incomplete Keep execution non-terminal and expose accountable residual state.

receipts.

32  Planner Agent Architecture
The Planner converts an admitted objective into a versioned execution graph of 
bounded tasks, dependencies, evidence needs, tools, models, agents, human gates, 
budgets, and stopping conditions. Planning proposes work; it does not grant capabilities 
or approve effects.
Figure 33. Planner Agent Architecture. The diagram organizes planning input, plan, plan revision, plan quality as one governed AI 
architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Planning input Task contract, objectives, constraints, known state, context policy, available capabilities, deadlines, 
budgets, and consequence.
Plan Directed acyclic or explicitly iterative graph of typed steps, owners, inputs, outputs, dependencies, 
guards, gates, retries, and completion criteria.
Plan revision Versioned change with reason, evidence, affected tasks, budget, capability, deadline, and 
supervisor approval.
Plan quality Completeness, feasibility, policy compliance, evidence coverage, critical path, uncertainty, 
reversibility, and expected value.
Normative requirements

33  Supervisor Agent Architecture
The Supervisor is the runtime authority for an admitted multi-agent execution. It 
validates plans, assigns bounded work, monitors progress and risk, challenges results, 
enforces budgets and gates, suspends unsafe activity, and determines terminal state 
under policy.
Figure 34. Supervisor Agent Architecture. The diagram organizes supervision scope, admission decision, runtime control, 
supervision evidence as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Supervision scope One execution or declared portfolio with task, agent, model, context, tool, budget, deadline, 
consequence, and gate boundaries.
Admission decision Approve, constrain, return, reject, or escalate a plan or revision based on policy and fitness.
Runtime control Assign, pause, resume, challenge, reroute, reduce scope, request review, terminate, compensate, 
or escalate.
Supervision evidence Decisions, signals, reasons, policy, evaluated state, budgets, dissent, interventions, and terminal 
rationale.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-33-001
Every multi-agent execution SHALL have exactly one 
accountable Supervisor identity and policy profile at any 
execution instant.

conformance test

for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.

The Supervisor SHALL validate plan, agent, model, context, tool, 
capability, budget, human-gate, quality, and safety closure before 
dispatch.
Security / privacy 
test
Signed adversarial result bound to 
task, artifacts, data class, provider, 
deployment, and policy.
AI-33-003

uncertainty, disagreement, tool effects, policy, budget, deadlines, 
loops, anomalies, and product fitness throughout execution.
Evaluation / 
monitoring
Reproducible profile, evidence set, 
slice metrics, thresholds, uncertainty, 
reviewer, and fitness decision.
AI-33-004
The Supervisor SHALL remain independently authorized from 
worker agents and SHALL NOT rely on worker self-report alone 
for capability, side-effect, safety, or completion state.

test
Signed adversarial result bound to 
task, artifacts, data class, provider, 
deployment, and policy.

products, reconciled effects, closed gates, complete audit, and

34  Workflow Agent and Durable Orchestration
The Workflow Agent mediates between agent reasoning and the durable workflow 
runtime. It translates approved plans into versioned workflow definitions and 
commands, tracks machine and human states, enforces timers and compensation, and 
exposes institutional progress independently of transient agent processes.
Figure 35. Workflow Agent and Durable Orchestration. The diagram organizes workflow projection, workflow command, human task, 
reconciliation as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Workflow projection Approved plan mapped to durable states, transitions, roles, tasks, gates, timers, retries, 
compensation, evidence, and terminal conditions.
Workflow command Authenticated, idempotent request to transition one instance under current preconditions and policy.
Human task Durable assignment with authority, exact context, choices, deadline, escalation, and completion 
evidence.

36  Tool, Capability, Sandbox, and Side-Effect 
Architecture
Tools expose institutional capability to agents through typed gateways and least-
privilege grants. Sandboxes isolate execution; capability policy limits data, network, 
compute, storage, models, credentials, and effects; human gates bind approval to exact 
consequential actions.
Figure 37. Tool, Capability, Sandbox, and Side-Effect Architecture. The diagram organizes tool contract, capability grant, sandbox, 
effect classes as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION

idempotency, compensation, timeout, and audit.
Capability grant Agent, execution, task, purpose, actions, object scope, environment, destinations, resources, 
duration, obligations, and revocation.
Sandbox Isolated process, filesystem, network, secret, package, compute, memory, storage, and persistence 
boundary.
Effect classes None, reversible, compensatable, approval-required, irreversible, prohibited, or unknown requiring 
restrictive classification.

38  Agent Context, Memory, Checkpointing, and 
Replay
Agent continuity is built from immutable context manifests, durable checkpoints, typed 
messages, workflow state, and external receipts. The runtime reconstructs what an 
agent knew and did without relying on hidden process memory, provider-retained 
sessions, or mutable conversation state.
Figure 39. Agent Context, Memory, Checkpointing, and Replay. The diagram organizes run context, checkpoint, run memory, replay 
as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Run context Admitted task, plan, packages, models, prompts, context, policies, capabilities, messages, tools, 
budgets, and time.
Checkpoint Durable execution state plus completed effects, pending tasks, dependencies, resource, 
uncertainty, and resume preconditions.
Run memory Bounded observations, intermediate products, reviews, tool results, and rationale necessary for the 
current execution.
Replay Deterministic or semantically bounded reconstruction of execution state, decisions, effects, 
products, and controls from preserved evidence.

43  Named Entity Recognition and Identity Resolution 
Agents
The NER Agent detects mentions and attributes in evidence; the Identity Resolution 
Agent maps them to candidate canonical entities. The pair preserves text spans, source 
context, ontology, ambiguity, confidence, temporal validity, and stewardship rather than 
treating a recognized name as a resolved identity.

evidence, product as one governed AI architecture contract.

organizations, people, technologies, and domain entities.

unresolved outcomes.
Evidence Exact source span or region, surrounding context, language, model and rule versions, confidence, 
and transformation lineage.
Product Entity mention and resolution proposal consumed by Knowledge Graph stewardship; never an 
unreviewed canonical identity write.

Agent searches for counter-evidence, alternative explanations, and failure conditions.

limitations, and accountable review.

47  Governance, Evaluation, and Learning Agents
Governance, Evaluation, and Learning Agents prepare control evidence and change 
proposals. They monitor conformance, execute approved evaluation profiles, connect 
outcomes to prior AI products, and propose improvements while remaining unable to 
approve their own risk or release production changes.
Figure 48. Governance, Evaluation, and Learning Agents. The diagram organizes governance agent, evaluation agent, learning 
agent, authority as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Governance Agent Monitors artifact status, authority, policy, exceptions, marketplace, documentation, reviews, and 
expiry and prepares governance cases.
Evaluation Agent Runs approved suites, metrics, slices, judges, challenge sets, and reproducibility checks and 
produces evidence, not approval.
Learning Agent Links outcomes, corrections, feedback, overrides, incidents, and drift to lesson proposals and 
candidate changes.
Authority Named governance, risk, evaluation, product, data, security, and release owners decide fitness, 
exceptions, and production change.
Normative requirements

48  Prediction Agent
The Prediction Agent produces calibrated probabilistic Forecast Packages for the 
canonical 30-day, 90-day, 180-day, one-year, three-year, and five-year horizons. It 
separates target definition, data, model, assumptions, distribution, drivers, uncertainty, 
sensitivity, validation, and failure conditions.
Figure 49. Prediction Agent. The diagram organizes forecast task, method, forecast package, lifecycle as one governed AI 
architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Forecast task Target, population, outcome definition, horizon, issue time, cutoff, context, decision use, refresh, 
and consequence.
Method Approved statistical, machine-learning, causal, rules, market-implied, expert, ensemble, or hybrid 
model composition.
Forecast Package Probability distribution, point summaries, intervals, drivers, calibration, sensitivity, scenarios, 
lineage, expiry, and failure conditions.
Lifecycle Requested, produced, reviewed, active, refreshed, expired, challenged, withdrawn, or superseded.
Normative requirements

conformance test

49  Scenario Agent
The Scenario Agent creates and maintains multiple coherent plausible future branches. 
It combines baseline state, actors, drivers, assumptions, critical uncertainties, signposts, 
interventions, impacts, and review without assigning destiny to one branch or disguising 
scenarios as probability forecasts.
Figure 50. Scenario Agent. The diagram organizes scenario task, scenario branch, portfolio, monitoring as one governed AI 
architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Scenario task Question, objectives, baseline, horizon, scope, actors, drivers, uncertainties, branch policy, and 
decision use.
Scenario Branch Versioned future with assumptions, causal or narrative logic, events, actor behavior, signposts, 
interventions, impacts, and uncertainty.
Portfolio Baseline, upside, downside, disruption, and governed custom branches with diversity, coherence, 
relevance, and coverage.
Monitoring Signposts, thresholds, evidence, branch relevance, review cadence, retirement, and promotion to 
simulation or decision.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-49-001
The Scenario Agent SHALL preserve multiple coherent branches 
and SHALL NOT collapse a forecast distribution, model sample, 
or favored narrative into a single future.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied, 
observable, and audited.
AI-49-002
Every branch SHALL bind baseline graph and twin state, 
assumptions, drivers, actors, mechanisms, uncertainties, 
signposts, interventions, impacts, evidence, and versions.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.
AI-49-003
Scenario probability, where used, SHALL be separately governed 
and SHALL NOT be inferred from narrative plausibility or agent 
preference.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied, 
observable, and audited.
AI-49-004
Branches SHALL be evaluated for internal coherence, 
distinctiveness, relevance, coverage, stereotype or political bias, 
and sensitivity to material assumptions.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.
AI-49-005
Observed signposts and changed evidence SHALL update 
branch relevance through versioned review while preserving prior 
scenario and decision context.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
rationale, reservations, and time.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Branches converge materially Revise portfolio diversity or expose insufficient alternative coverage.
Scenario logic incoherent Mark failed coherence and prohibit promotion to simulation or decision use.

50  Simulation Agent
The Simulation Agent prepares, executes, validates, and interprets reproducible 
experiments over exact twin, scenario, model, policy, environment, intervention, 
constraint, and seed versions. It distinguishes simulated outcomes from observed reality 
and from probability forecasts.
Figure 51. Simulation Agent. The diagram organizes experiment manifest, execution, simulation run, interpretation as one governed 
AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experiment manifest Objective, initial state, twin and scenario, models, rules, agents, interventions, constraints, 
environment, seeds, outputs, budgets, and validation.
Execution Isolated run or ensemble with exact artifacts, scheduler, resource, random state, checkpoints, 
telemetry, and failure state.
Simulation Run Outputs, distributions, trajectories, impacts, sensitivity, uncertainty, validity, limitations, resource, 
and reproducibility evidence.
Interpretation Assumption-bound explanation of results, not a claim that the world will behave identically.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
AI-50-001
The agent SHALL validate complete experiment manifests and 
SHALL reject unresolved artifact, state, constraint, policy, budget, 
or expected-output dependencies.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.
AI-50-002
Simulation execution SHALL occur in isolated synthetic branches 
and SHALL never write outcomes into observed twin or canonical 
world state.
Evaluation / 
monitoring
Reproducible profile, evidence set, 
slice metrics, thresholds, uncertainty, 
reviewer, and fitness decision.
AI-50-003
Runs SHALL preserve seeds, nondeterministic components, 
environment, scheduler, model, agent, policy, configuration, data, 
and hardware-relevant versions required for reproducibility.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.
AI-50-004
Simulation products SHALL expose validity domain, 
assumptions, uncertainty, sensitivity, convergence, rare events, 
model discrepancy, and validation against observed or 
benchmark behavior.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact task, artifacts, context, 
configuration, and environment.
AI-50-005
Interpretation SHALL preserve alternative explanations and 
SHALL not convert simulated frequency into calibrated real-world 
probability without an approved mapping and evaluation.
Evaluation / 
monitoring
Reproducible profile, evidence set, 
slice metrics, thresholds, uncertainty, 
reviewer, and fitness decision.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Manifest incomplete Reject before resource allocation and identify missing dependencies.
Run non-reproducible Mark result unfit, preserve evidence, and investigate nondeterministic or environment drift.

51  Decision Agent
The Decision Agent assembles a complete Decision Package for human authority. It 
frames the question, connects objectives and constraints, develops alternatives, 
integrates evidence and foresight, exposes trade-offs and dissent, and proposes 
monitoring and commitments without making the final decision.
Figure 52. Decision Agent. The diagram organizes decision frame, options, decision package, authority boundary as one governed 
AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Decision frame Question, accountable human authority, scope, objectives, constraints, stakeholders, deadline, 
reversibility, and consequence.
Options Status quo and actionable alternatives with assumptions, dependencies, resources, impacts, risks, 
opportunities, reversibility, and information gaps.
Decision Package Evidence, assessments, forecasts, scenarios, simulations, uncertainty, trade-offs, dissent, policy, 
recommendation, explanation, and monitoring.
Authority boundary Agent may recommend and prepare; named human approves, rejects, changes, defers, delegates, 
abstains, or reopens.

investment, options, and owners.

warnings, decisions, and responses.
Portfolio Deduplicated and connected exposures with dependencies, concentration, optionality, correlation, 
and transparent prioritization.

Executive Briefing and Reporting Agents render governed strategic products for specific

contract, publication product as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract

57  Weak Signal and Early Warning Agents
Weak Signal and Early Warning Agents convert low-maturity change into governed 
attention. They combine novelty, mechanism, evidence, graph and twin exposure, 
forecasts, scenarios, objectives, consequence, urgency, reversibility, and information 
value without inflating confidence or flooding operators.

AI architecture contract

maturity, and maintains monitored candidates.
Early Warning Agent Evaluates materiality, deduplicates and prioritizes candidates, resolves accountable owners, issues 
Warning Products, and tracks lifecycle.
Warning Product Change, mechanism, evidence, counter-evidence, uncertainty, consequence, objectives, response 
window, owner, options, and closure.
Lifecycle Candidate, monitored, issued, acknowledged, investigating, acted, dismissed, expired, resolved, or 
superseded.

58  AI Risk Taxonomy and Consequence Classes
AI risk in The Eye is classified by the harm that a capability can enable, amplify, 
conceal, or fail to prevent. Consequence classification controls permissible models, 
agents, data, tools, autonomy, human gates, evaluation depth, monitoring, release 
authority, and recovery obligations for each task and product use.
Figure 59. AI Risk Taxonomy and Consequence Classes. The diagram organizes risk dimensions, consequence classes, 
classification object, control principle as one governed AI architecture contract.
AI architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Risk dimensions Decision harm, physical and cyber effect, financial exposure, rights and privacy, strategic 
misdirection, information integrity, security, availability, and institutional accountability.
Consequence classes C0 informational; C1 bounded analytical; C2 material advisory; C3 consequential decision support; 
C4 safety-, mission-, rights-, or system-critical.
Classification object Exact task, product, population, data, tool, deployment, user role, decision context, and reachable 
side effect—not a model family in isolation.
Control principle Controls accumulate with consequence, uncertainty, scale, irreversibility, vulnerability, novelty, and 
propagation potential.
Normative requirements
